Verify the side empirically instead of assuming.\n\n**Why:** observed. The corpus egocentric heuristic (+y = robot-left = image-right)\npredicts right = -y = image-left, but this cell's predicate evaluated the pudding\n\"to the right of the plate\" as satisfied at +y and unsatisfied at -y. The sign\nconvention of the underlying spatial predicate here is viewer/world +y = \"right\",\nnot robot-egocentric. Do not over-generalize either sign.\n**How to apply:** When a clean placement of the CORRECT object on your guessed\nside does not terminate (and the eef is retreated clear, see related), flip to\nthe OTHER side before suspecting the co-clause (e.g. the On() half). Budget one\nprobe per new seed: place, retreat clear, check; if false and geometry is clean,\ntry the opposite y-sign. Keep the co-object's placement fixed while flipping.\n**Falsify:** A libero_10 left/right cell that fires on the egocentric side\n(right=-y), confirming the egocentric rule for that task \u2014 record it as a\ncounter-example so the sign is treated as task-specific, not global.\n**Related:** [[predicate-gated-by-eef-proximity-retreat-clear]]\n"

On a\nwide-mouth mug the two fingers straddle the rim and enter the cavity, so closing\ngrips air (finger separation stays ~0.06, no lift). The thin side handle is a\nsolid, narrow feature pi0 can pinch (finger separation closes to ~0.01).\n**How to apply:** Pre-position ~15-20 cm above the mug, offset the eef slightly\ntoward the handle side / +y, then `pi0_pick \"grasp the {color} mug by the handle\"`\nwith max_chunks 12-18. Confirm grip_opening ~0.01 and peak_lift > 0.15. pi0 often\nalso auto-carries the eef forward after lifting \u2014 that is fine; firm with\nset_gripper +1 and take over the carry. The mug then hangs with its base offset\n~(+0.05,-0.03 m) from the eef; place by putting eef = target - that offset and\ndescending until OSC stalls (base contact).\n**Falsify:** A tall mug that pi0 lifts cleanly from the plain top-center prompt\n(narrow mouth), or a handle-grasp that fails to close on the handle.\n**Related:** [[predicate-gated-by-eef-proximity-retreat-clear]]\n"

a) Try to place with eef y ≈ 0.29 (offset mug center at 0.25, i.e., 3cm short of the plate center 0.278). The mug base radius is ~3.5cm and the plate radius ~7cm, so the mug would still be mostly on the plate. The On() predicate typically checks the object's center within the plate's... hmm, in LIBERO the On predicate uses a contact/height check: it checks that the object A's bottom is on top of B, implemented via contact or a bounding check — in libero the `On` predicate checks that object A's z position is greater than B's and their xy overlap... Actually LIBERO's On predicate uses `contact` between A and B with a normal pointing up? Let me recall: libero predicate `On` implemented as: obj A pos z > obj B pos z, and the xy distance within a threshold based on sampling. Specifically LIBERO's `On` predicate: `check_contact(self, object1, object2)` → they check contact via `contact` points and additionally the relative position. Hmm.

In LIBERO the predicate is:
```
class On(Predicate):
    def __call__(self, state, _):
        return self._eval_predicate(state)
```
which uses "sample_check" of contact between the objects, plus `object z` comparison. There's also a "sanity" check that objects are in contact and the upper object's bottom is above.

Given uncertainty, the safest is to get the mug as centered on the plate as possible. But there's another factor: the mug is tilted in the hand, so it lands tilted — after release the mug might settle with its base partly off.

b) Use rotate_wrist (yaw) to rotate the payload 90°, which redirects the offset into x. Then eef y = plate_y = 0.278, still barely reachable (0.278 is under 0.30). Memory s0: they rotated +90° yaw then move_pose at the actual yaw, reaching eef y=+0.3118 with stalls.

Hmm, but rotating the yaw of a handle-grasped mug just spins it around; the offset direction rotates with it. If I rotate yaw by +90° (about world z, the gripper rotates), the mug's offset (0.015,-0.040) rotates: rotating a vector (0.015,-0.040) by +90° (CCW): (x,y) → (-y, x) = (0.040, 0.015). So the offset becomes mostly +x → eef = (plate_x - 0.040, plate_y - 0.015) = (-0.058, 0.263). That's comfortably reachable!
